'Brainwidemap'\n    40→\n    41→T_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\n    42→BIN = 0.02\n    43→N_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\n    44→EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\n    45→TIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n    46→\n    47→# reaction time bounds of the paper, which truncates the distribution at 80 ms and 2 s;\n    48→MIN_RT, MAX_RT = 0.08, 2.0\n    49→\n    50→QC_LABEL = 1.0                   # the \"stringent quality control\" of the paper, 75,708 units of 621,733\n    51→SPLIT = [100 / 3, 2 * 100 / 3]       # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n    52→\n    53→CHOICE = {1.0: 0, -1.0: 1}       # +1 is a leftward choice, -1 rightward; 0 is a no response, dropped\n    54→PRIOR = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n    55→MOVEMENT = ['low', 'medium', 'high']\n    56→\n    57→INPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_stimulus_onset', 'trial_number_in_block']\n    58→OUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy']\n    59→OUTPUT_VALUES = [['left', 'right'], ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'], MOVEMENT, MOVEMENT]\n    60→\n    61→# the cache location is not known until --datadir is parsed, and the connection cannot be\n    62→# shared across a fork anyway, so connect() fills this in per process rather than at import\n    63→one = None\n    64→brain_regions = BrainRegions()\n    65→\n    66→\n    67→def connect(datadir):\n    68→    \"\"\"Point the module-level ONE client at the cache under datadir.\n    69→\n    70→    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    71→    Sets the global `one`; returns nothing.\n    72→    \"\"\"\n    73→    global one\n    74→    one = ONE(base_url='https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org', password='international',\n    75→              silent=True, cache_dir=os.path.join(datadir, 'one_cache'))\n    76→\n    77→\n    78→# --- sessions ---\n    79→\n    80→def select_sessions():\n    81→    \"\"\"Released sessions that have everything the decoder needs, grouped by subject.\"\"\"\n    82→    one.load_cache(tag=RELEASE)               # downloads the release index if it is not cached\n    83→\n    84→    required = ['spikes.times.npy', 'spikes.clusters.npy',          # ephys\n    85→                '_ibl_trials.table.pqt',                            # task\n    86→                '_ibl_wheel.position.npy', '_ibl_wheel.timestamps.npy']\n    87→\n    88→    keep = set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=required))\n    89→    keep &= (set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['leftCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n    90→                                                          '_ibl_leftCamera.times.npy']))\n    91→             | set(one.search(query_type='local', datasets=['rightCamera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n    92→                                                            '_ibl_rightCamera.times.npy'])))\n    93→\n    94→    eids, info = one.search(query_type='local', details=True)\n    95→    selected = {}\n    96→    for eid, session in zip(eids, info):\n    97→        if eid in keep:\n    98→            selected.setdefault(session['subject'], []).append(str(eid))\n    99→    return {subject: selected[subject] for subject in sorted(selected)}\n   100→\n   101→\n   102→def camera_view(eid):\n   103→    \"\"\"The side camera this session has whisker motion energy for, left preferred.\"\"\"\n   104→    for view in ('left', 'right'):\n   105→        if len(one.list_datasets(eid, filename=f'{view}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n   106→                                 query_type='local')):\n   107→            return view\n   108→    raise ValueError(f'no whisker motion energy for {eid}')\n   109→\n   110→\n   111→# --- trials ---\n   112→\n   113→def trial_variables(trials):\n   114→    \"\"\"Choice, block prior and trial number in block, one row per trial.\"\"\"\n   115→    # probabilityLeft is constant within a block, so a change of value starts a new block\n   116→    block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n   117→\n   118→    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n   119→                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n   120→                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n   121→\n   122→\n   123→def covered(times, stim_on):\n   124→    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n   125→    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n   126→    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n   127→    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n   128→\n   129→    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n   130→    inside = begin <= end\n   131→    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n   132→    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n   133→    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n   134→\n   135→\n   136→def usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n   137→    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n   138→    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n   139→    reaction = trials.firstMovement_times.to_numpy() - stim_on\n   140→\n   141→    keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n   142→    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n   143→    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n   144→\n   145→    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n   146→\n   147→\n   148→# --- behavior ---\n   149→\n   150→def window_slice(times, stim_on):\n   151→    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n   152→    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n   153→    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n   154→\n   155→    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n   156→    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n   157→    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n   158→\n   159→    return begin, end\n   160→\n   161→\n   162→def trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n   163→    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n   164→    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n   165→\n   166→    traces = []\n   167→    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n   168→        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n   169→        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n   170→        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n   171→        last = end[trial] + 1\n   172→\n   173→        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n   174→\n   175→    return np.array(traces)\n   176→\n   177→\n   178→def discretize(trace):\n   179→    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n   180→    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n   181→\n   182→\n   183→# --- neural ---\n   184→\n   185→def load_neural(eid):\n   186→    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n   187→    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n   188→    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n   189→        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n   190→\n   191→        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n   192→        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n   193→\n   194→        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n   195→        if not clusters:\n   196→            continue\n   197→\n   198→        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n   199→\n   200→        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n   201→        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n   202→        is_good = clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL\n   203→        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n   204→\n   205→        # renumber the surviving clusters, continuing the count from the previous probe\n   206→        new_index = np.cumsum(is_good) - 1\n   207→        units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n   208→        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n   209→\n   210→        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. DG-sg and DG-mo both become DG\n   211→        regions.extend(brain_regions.acronym2acronym(clusters['acronym'][is_good], Harbor mounts the dataset at /app/data while\n# this script runs from /solution, so solve.sh overrides this with --datadir.\nDATA_DIR = os.path.join(HERE, 'data')\nRELEASE = 'Brainwidemap'\n\nT_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\nBIN = 0.02\nN_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\nEDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\nTIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n\nCHOICE = {1.0: 0, -1.0: 1}       # +1 is a leftward choice, -1 rightward; 0 is a no response, dropped\nPRIOR = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\nMOVEMENT = ['low', 'medium', 'high']\n\nINPUT_NAMES = ['time_from_stimulus_onset', 'trial_number_in_block']\nOUTPUT_NAMES = ['choice', 'prior_probability_left', 'wheel_speed', 'whisker_motion_energy']\nOUTPUT_VALUES = [['left', 'right'], ['0.2', '0.5', '0.8'], MOVEMENT, MOVEMENT]\n\n# the cache location is not known until --datadir is parsed, and the connection cannot be\n# shared across a fork anyway, so connect() fills this in per process rather than at import\none = None\nbrain_regions = BrainRegions()\n\n\ndef connect(datadir):\n    \"\"\"Point the module-level ONE client at the cache under datadir.\n\n    datadir: directory holding the one_cache/ folder.\n    Sets the global `one`; returns nothing.\n    \"\"\"\n    global one\n    one = ONE(base_url='https://openalyx.internationalbrainlab.org', password='international',\n              silent=True, cache_dir=os.path.join(datadir, 'one_cache'))\n\n\n# --- sessions ---\n\ndef select_sessions():\n    \"\"\"Released sessions that have everything the decoder needs, grouped by subject.\"\"\"\n    one.load_cache(tag=RELEASE)               # probabilityLeft is constant within a block, so a change of value starts a new block\n    block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n\n    return pd.DataFrame({'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n                         'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n                         'trial_in_block': trials.groupby(block).cumcount()})\n\n\ndef covered(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Trials whose window is spanned by this stream, allowing the one bin gap the reference allows.\"\"\"\n    start, stop = stim_on + T_START, stim_on + T_STOP\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, start, side='right')      # first sample inside the window\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, stop, side='left') - 1       # last sample inside the window\n\n    # a trial with no sample at all, or a gap at either edge, has no usable trace\n    inside = begin <= end\n    first = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(begin, 0, times.size - 1)], np.inf)\n    last = np.where(inside, times[np.clip(end, 0, times.size - 1)], -np.inf)\n    return inside & (np.abs(start - first) <= BIN) & (np.abs(stop - last) <= BIN)\n\n\ndef usable_trials(trials, wheel_times, camera_times):\n    \"\"\"Trials the paper would keep, that also have a full wheel and camera window.\"\"\"\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()\n    reaction = trials.firstMovement_times.to_numpy() - stim_on\n\n    keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n    keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\n    return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n\n\n# --- behavior ---\n\ndef window_slice(times, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Index range of each trial window, so a stream is sliced rather than rescanned per trial.\"\"\"\n    window_start = stim_on + T_START\n    window_stop = stim_on + T_STOP\n\n    # the samples are in time order, so each window is one contiguous range of them\n    begin = np.searchsorted(times, window_start)\n    end = np.searchsorted(times, window_stop)\n\n    return begin, end\n\n\ndef trial_traces(times, values, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"A continuous trace resampled onto the same time grid for every trial.\"\"\"\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    traces = []\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        # one sample either side of the window, so the first and last bin centre are\n        # interpolated rather than clamped to the nearest sample inside it\n        first = max(begin[trial] - 1, 0)\n        last = end[trial] + 1\n\n        traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n\n    return np.array(traces)\n\n\ndef discretize(trace):\n    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n\n\n# --- neural ---\n\ndef load_neural(eid):\n    \"\"\"Spike times and good clusters of a session, merged over its probes.\"\"\"\n    times, units, regions = [], [], []\n    for pid, name in zip(*one.eid2pid(eid)):\n        loader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n\n        # skip the md5 check, which rereads every spike file and draws its own progress bar\n        spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n\n        # a session can hold an insertion whose spike sorting was never released\n        if not clusters:\n            continue\n\n        clusters = loader.merge_clusters(spikes, clusters, channels)   # attaches metrics and histology\n\n        # spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n        # can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n        is_good = clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL\n        keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n\n        # renumber the surviving clusters, continuing the count from the previous probe\n        new_index = np.cumsum(is_good) - 1\n        units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n        times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n\n        # Beryl is the coarser atlas the reference code uses, e.g. DG-sg and DG-mo both become DG\n        regions.extend(brain_regions.acronym2acronym(clusters['acronym'][is_good], mapping='Beryl'))\n\n    times, units = np.concatenate(times), np.concatenate(units)\n\n    # sort the merged probes by spike time, as the reference does, so trials can be sliced\n    order = np.argsort(times, kind='stable')\n    return times[order], units[order], np.array(regions)\n\n\ndef spike_counts(times, units, n_units, stim_on):\n    \"\"\"Spike count per unit and bin, for every trial.\"\"\"\n    counts = np.empty((stim_on.size, n_units, N_BINS))\n    begin, end = window_slice(times, stim_on)\n\n    for trial, onset in enumerate(stim_on):\n        spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # one flat index per spike lets a single bincount fill the whole unit by bin grid\n        flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n        counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n\n    return counts\n\n\n# --- session ---\n\ndef process_session(eid, subject):\n    \"\"\"One session as lists of per trial neural, input and output arrays.\"\"\"\n    loader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\n    loader.load_trials()\n    loader.load_wheel()\n    view = camera_view(eid)\n    loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n\n    trials, wheel = loader.trials, loader.wheel\n    motion_energy = loader.motion_energy[f'{view}Camera']\n\n    variables = trial_variables(trials)                 # before filtering, so the block count is real\n    keep = usable_trials(trials, wheel.times.to_numpy(), motion_energy.times.to_numpy())\n    variables = variables[keep]\n    stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()[keep]\n\n    # no trial survived the mask, so there is nothing to load or bin\n    if stim_on.size == 0:\n        return {'session_id': str(eid), 'subject': subject, 'camera': view,\n                'neural': [], 'input': [], 'output': [],\n                'n_trials': 0, 'n_units': 0, 'regions': np.array([])}\n\n    speed = trial_traces(wheel.times, np.abs(wheel.velocity), stim_on)\n    whisker = trial_traces(motion_energy.times, motion_energy.iloc[:, 1], stim_on)\n\n    spike_times, spike_units, regions = load_neural(eid)\n    rate = spike_counts(spike_times, spike_units, regions.size, stim_on) / BIN\n\n    # the split is over the whole session, so both traces are discretized once, not per trial\n    speed_class = discretize(speed)\n    whisker_class = discretize(whisker)\n\n    neural = [trial.astype(np.float32) for trial in rate]\n    inputs, outputs = [], []\n    for trial in range(stim_on.size):\n        row = variables.iloc[trial]\n        inputs.append(np.stack([TIME, np.full(N_BINS, row.trial_in_block)]).astype(np.float32))\n        outputs.append(np.stack([np.full(N_BINS, row.choice), np.full(N_BINS, row.prior),\n                                 speed_class[trial], whisker_class[trial]]).astype(np.int8))\n\n    return {'session_id': str(eid), 'subject': subject, 'camera': view,\n            'neural': neural, 'input': inputs, 'output': outputs,\n            'n_trials': len(neural), 'n_units': regions.size, 'regions': regions}\n\n\ndef assemble(results):\n    \"\"\"Stitch per-session results into the target dictionary.\"\"\"\n    subjects = sorted({r['subject'] for r in results})\n    sub_ix = {s: i for i, s in enumerate(subjects)}\n\n    regions = sorted({region for r in results for region in r['regions']})\n    reg_ix = {region: i for i, region in enumerate(regions)}\n\n    return {\n        'neural': [r['neural'] for r in results],\n        'input': [r['input'] for r in results],\n        'output': [r['output'] for r in results],\n\n        'subjects': subjects,\n        'subject_idx': np.array([sub_ix[r['subject']] for r in results], np.int32),\n\n        'brain_regions': regions,\n        'brain_region_idx': [np.array([reg_ix[x] for x in r['regions']], np.int32) for r in results],\n\n        'input_names': INPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_names': OUTPUT_NAMES,\n        'output_values': OUTPUT_VALUES,\n\n        'metadata': {\n            'task_description':\n                'Head-fixed mice turn a wheel to report the side of a visual grating. The '\n                'probability that the stimulus appears on the left is held constant within a '\n                'block and switches between 0.2 and 0.8.',\n            'time_bin_size': BIN * 1000.0,          # sessions are independent, so they are processed out of order and put back by index\n    ordered = [None] * len(pairs)\n    with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.workers, initializer=worker_setup,\n                             initargs=(args.datadir,)) as pool:\n        futures = {pool.submit(process_session, eid, subject): index\n                   for index, (eid, subject) in enumerate(pairs)}\n\n        progress = tqdm(as_completed(futures), total=len(futures), unit='session')\n        for future in progress:\n            eid, subject = pairs[futures[future]]\n            try:\n                res = future.result()\n            except Exception as error:\n                print('failed %s: %s: %s' % (eid[:8], type(error).__name__, error), flush=True)\n                continue\n\n            # a session with no surviving unit or trial will be skipped\n            if res['n_units'] == 0 or res['n_trials'] < 2:\n                print('skipped %s: %d trials, %d units'\n                      % (res['session_id'][:8], res['n_trials'], res['n_units']), flush=True)\n                continue\n\n            ordered[futures[future]] = res\n            progress.set_postfix_str('%s %s %d trials %d units'\n                                     % (res['session_id'][:8], subject, res['n_trials'], res['n_units']))\n\n    results = [res for res in ordered if res is not None]\n    data = assemble(results)\n    print('\\nconverted in %.1f s' % (time.time() - t0), flush=True)\n    print('sessions %d | subjects %d | regions %d | trials %d | units %d'\n          % (len(results), len(data['subjects']), len(data['brain_regions']),\n             sum(r['n_trials'] for r in results), sum(r['n_units'] for r in results)), flush=True)\n\n    with open(args.outfile, 'wb') as f:\n        pickle.dump(data, f, protocol=pickle.HIGHEST_PROTOCOL)\n    print('wrote %s (%.1f GB)' % (args.outfile, os.path.getsize(args.outfile) / 1e9), flush=True)\n\n\nif __name__ == '__main__':\n    main()\n","numLines":401,"startLine":1,"totalLines":401}}}

How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n     4→\n     5→i. Everything is read through the ONE api against the IBL cache in `data/one_cache`, never by opening files directly. The index of the brain-wide map release is loaded first, listing the sessions and the datasets each one holds. Searching that index returns an `eid`, the session identifier, and that one string is the only handle needed afterwards: every loader takes the `eid` and resolves the files itself. `SessionLoader` reads the trials table, the wheel and the camera motion energy of a session, and `SpikeSortingLoader` reads the spikes and clusters of one probe insertion, so it is called once per probe.\n     6→\n     7→ii. The API resolves a session and a dataset name to a path. Use the one.search to idenity unique session id to then locate the data files.\n    20→\n    21→## 1-b. How are the data split into subjects?\n    22→\n    23→i. The search returns the subject name alongside each `eid`, so no parsing of paths or file names is involved. Sessions are collected into a dictionary keyed by subject, which is the form the conversion iterates over. At assembly the subjects are the sorted unique names and `subject_idx` records, for each session, its index into that list. The 444 converted sessions come from 136 subjects, a median of three sessions per animal and up to thirteen.\n    24→\n    25→ii. A session is already the unit the release is organised by, and the search returns one `eid` per session, so nothing has to be split.\n    41→\n    42→ii. The search returns the session identifiers:\n    43→```python\n    44→eids, info = one.search(query_type='local', details=True)\n    45→```\n    46→\n    47→iii. No decision to make, the API returns sessions individually.\n    48→\n    49→## 1-d. Are the data correctly split into trials?\n    50→\n    51→i. The trials table has one row per trial, so the split is given by the data.\n    52→\n    53→ii. The trials table of a session:\n    54→```python\n    55→loader.load_trials()\n    56→```\n    57→\n    58→iii. No decision to make, the trials table is already one row per trial.\n    59→\n    60→## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n    61→\n    62→i. Four things, all applied together. The reaction time, first wheel movement minus stimulus onset, has to fall between 80 ms and 2 s. Trials where the animal made no choice are dropped. Trials whose choice or block probability is missing are dropped. Finally the trial window has to be spanned by the wheel and the camera, which drops trials at the edges of a recording.\n    63→\n    64→ii.\n    65→```python\n    66→keep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\n    67→keep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\n    68→keep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n    69→\n    70→return keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n    71→```\n    72→\n    73→iii. The reaction time bounds and the no-response exclusion are the reference paper's own trial mask, `min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2` with `exclude_nochoice=True`, and the data paper truncates its reaction time distribution at the same 80 ms and 2 s.\n    74→\n    75→## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the final `neural` data derived from?\n    76→\n    77→i. Two arrays per probe, `spikes.times` and `spikes.clusters`, which are the time of every spike and the cluster it was assigned to. The cluster table supplies the quality label and the anatomical location, but the neural array itself is built only from those two.\n    78→\n    79→ii. Both come from the spike sorting loader:\n    80→```python\n    81→spikes, clusters, channels = loader.load_spike_sorting(check_hash=False)\n    82→```\n    83→\n    84→```python\n    85→units.append(new_index[spikes['clusters'][keep]] + len(regions))\n    86→times.append(spikes['times'][keep])\n    87→```\n    88→\n    89→iii. Spike time is directly avialible through the loader.\n    90→\n    91→## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n    92→\n    93→i. Spikes are counted into 20 ms bins over the trial window, giving one count per unit per bin, and the counts are divided by the bin width to become a firing rate in Hz. When a session has two probes their units are pooled into one population, numbered continuously so that the second probe's units continue after the first probe's.\n    94→\n    95→ii. Counting a trial, then converting to a rate:\n    96→```python\n    97→bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n    98→flat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\n    99→counts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n   100→```\n   101→\n   102→```python\n   103→rate = spike_counts(spike_times, spike_units, regions.size, stim_on) / BIN\n   104→```\n   105→\n   106→iii. The reference bins spike counts and does not smooth, so no smoothing is applied here either. Merging the probes of a session follows the reference's `merge_probes`, whose reasoning is that two probes in one session see the same behaviour and so are not independent recordings.\n   107→\n   108→## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n   109→\n   110→i. Only clusters whose `label` is 1 are kept. The label is a score of 0, 1/3, 2/3 or 1 that the IBL pipeline assigns from a set of spike sorting metrics, so requiring 1 keeps the units that pass all of them. Across the release this keeps 75,708 of 621,733 clusters, and in the converted data 73,044 units over 444 sessions, a median of 142 per session.\n   111→\n   112→ii. The filter, applied per probe before the spikes are kept:\n   113→```python\n   114→# spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n   115→# can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\n   116→is_good = clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL\n   117→keep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n   118→```\n   119→\n   120→iii. This is the curation of the data paper, which reports 75,708 well-isolated neurons averaging 108 per probe; applying `label >= 1` reproduce their number.\n   121→\n   122→## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n   123→\n   124→i. Every stream of a session is already expressed in seconds on one shared clock: spike times, trial event times, wheel timestamps and camera frame times all run from the start of the recording, because IBL synchronises them upstream through a sync channel common to the ephys and the behaviour rig. Aligning a trial is therefore just subtracting its stimulus onset from the spike times, which puts zero at the event and leaves the window running from -0.5 s to 1.5 s around it.\n   125→\n   126→ii. Subtracting the onset:\n   127→```python\n   128→spike_time = times[begin[trial]:end[trial]] - onset      # time from stimulus onset\n   129→```\n   130→\n   131→iii. Because the clocks are already aligned there is additional alignment required.\n   132→\n   133→## 2-e. How is the `neural` data temporally binned/resampled?\n   134→\n   135→i. The 2 s window is divided into 100 bins of 20 ms; no resample or interpolation is applied.\n   136→\n   137→ii. The bin grid, and the bin each spike falls in:\n   138→```python\n   139→BIN = 0.02\n   140→N_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\n   141→```\n   142→\n   143→```python\n   144→bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n   145→```\n   146→\n   147→iii. The reference code sets `'binsize': 0.02`, and both papers use 20 ms bins; the method paper describes exactly this configuration, \"split into 2-s trials, each divided into 20-ms bins, producing T = 100 time steps\".\n   148→\n   149→## 3-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *time_from_stimulus_onset* derived from?\n   150→\n   151→i. From `stimOn_times` in the trials table, which is the event every trial is aligned on. The window is -0.5 to 1.5 s around that onset in 20 ms bins, and the input is the centre of each of the 100 bins, so one set of values serves every trial.\n   152→\n   153→ii. The onset each trial is aligned on, and the window taken around it:\n   154→```python\n   155→stim_on = trials.stimOn_times.to_numpy()[keep]\n   156→```\n   157→```python\n   158→T_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\n   159→BIN = 0.02\n   160→N_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\n   161→EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\n   162→TIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n   163→```\n   164→\n   165→iii. The window and the bin size are the decoding parameters of the reference code.\n   166→\n   167→## 3-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *time_from_stimulus_onset*?\n   168→\n   169→i. There is no processing, the variable is defined by us.\n   170→\n   171→ii. The centres, and the input of one trial built from them:\n   172→```python\n   173→T_START, T_STOP = -0.5, 1.5      # decoding window around stimulus onset, following the reference code\n   174→BIN = 0.02\n   175→N_BINS = int(round((T_STOP - T_START) / BIN))            # 100\n   176→EDGES = T_START + BIN * np.arange(N_BINS + 1)\n   177→TIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n   178→```\n   179→\n   180→iii. N/A\n   181→\n   182→## 3-c. How is `input` *time_from_stimulus_onset* aligned with the neural data?\n   183→\n   184→i. It is the neural binning grid itself: the spikes are binned on `EDGES` around each trial's `stimOn_times`, and the input is the centre of those same bins, so bin for bin the two describe the same instant.\n   185→\n   186→ii. The grid the spikes are binned on, and the centres of it:\n   187→```python\n   188→bin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\n   189→```\n   190→```python\n   191→TIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2\n   192→```\n   193→\n   194→iii. N/A\n   195→\n   196→## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *trial_number_in_block* derived from?\n   197→\n   198→i. From `probabilityLeft` in the trials table, which is held constant within a block, so a change of its value starts a new one.\n   199→\n   200→ii. The blocks found from where the prior changes:\n   201→```python\n   202→block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n   203→```\n   204→\n   205→iii. The trials table carries no block identifier, so the blocks have to be recovered from the prior.\n   206→\n   207→## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *trial_number_in_block*?\n   208→\n   209→i. The value is the position of a trial within its block, counted from zero.\n   210→\n   211→ii. The count within each block, and the broadcast:\n   212→```python\n   213→block = (trials.probabilityLeft != trials.probabilityLeft.shift()).cumsum()\n   214→```\n   215→\n   216→iii. The count is taken before the reaction time and no response filters are applied, so a trial that is later dropped still advances it and the number is the animal's real position in the block.\n   217→\n   218→## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *choice* derived from?\n   219→\n   220→i. One column of the trials table, `choice`, which is +1, -1 or 0. It is recoded to 0 for left and 1 for right, and the 0 entries, meaning the animal did not respond, are dropped with the trial.\n   221→\n   222→ii. The mapping, and where it is applied:\n   223→```python\n   224→CHOICE = {1.0: 0, -1.0: 1}       # +1 is a leftward choice, -1 rightward; 0 is a no response, dropped\n   225→```\n   226→\n   227→```python\n   228→'choice': trials.choice.map(CHOICE),\n   229→```\n   230→\n   231→iii. The IBL uses +1 for left choice and -1 for rightward choice. No response trials are dropped.\n   232→\n   233→## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *choice*?\n   234→\n   235→i. None beyond the recoding of +1 and -1 to 0 and 1 described above.\n   236→\n   237→ii. N/A\n   238→\n   239→iii. N/A\n   240→\n   241→## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *prior_probability_left* derived from?\n   242→\n   243→i. One column of the trials table, `probabilityLeft`, which takes the three values 0.2, 0.5 and 0.8 and is recoded to 0, 1 and 2.\n   244→\n   245→ii. The mapping, and where it is applied:\n   246→```python\n   247→PRIOR = {0.2: 0, 0.5: 1, 0.8: 2}\n   248→```\n   249→\n   250→```python\n   251→'prior': trials.probabilityLeft.map(PRIOR),\n   252→```\n   253→\n   254→iii. The three values are the block prior the task holds constant within a block, and the instructions give this mapping.\n   255→\n   256→## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *prior_probability_left*?\n   257→\n   258→i. None beyond the recoding of 0.2, 0.5 and 0.8 to 0, 1 and 2 described above.\n   259→\n   260→ii. N/A\n   261→\n   262→iii. N/A\n   263→\n   264→## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *wheel_speed* derived from?\n   265→\n   266→i. The wheel position and its timestamps, `_ibl_wheel.position` and `_ibl_wheel.timestamps`. `SessionLoader` turns them into a velocity, and the speed is the absolute value of that velocity.\n   267→\n   268→ii. The wheel, and the speed taken from it:\n   269→```python\n   270→loader.load_wheel()\n   271→```\n   272→\n   273→```python\n   274→speed = trial_traces(wheel.times, np.abs(wheel.velocity), stim_on)\n   275→```\n   276→\n   277→iii. The reference derives `'wheel-speed'` the same way, as `np.abs` of the velocity that `SessionLoader` returns.\n   278→\n   279→## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *wheel_speed*?\n   280→\n   281→i. Three steps. The wheel is recorded only when it moves, so `SessionLoader` first interpolates the position onto an even 1000 Hz grid and differentiates it into a velocity, applying a 20 Hz Butterworth low pass while doing so; the speed is the absolute value of that velocity, in radians per second. That trace is then resampled onto the 100 bin centres of each trial. Finally it is cut into three classes at the 33rd and 67th percentile of the session, so the three classes are equally sized within a session.\n   282→\n   283→ii. What `SessionLoader.load_wheel` does internally:\n   284→```python\n   285→self.wheel['position'], self.wheel['times'] = interpolate_position(\n   286→    wheel_raw['timestamps'], wheel_raw['position'], freq=fs)\n   287→self.wheel['velocity'], self.wheel['acceleration'] = velocity_filtered(\n   288→    self.wheel['position'], fs=fs, corner_frequency=corner_frequency, order=order)\n   289→```\n   290→\n   291→Resampling onto the bin centres, then splitting into three:\n   292→```python\n   293→traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n   294→```\n   295→\n   296→```python\n   297→SPLIT = [100 / 3, 2 * 100 / 3]       # equal sized classes, from percentiles of each session's own trace\n   298→\n   299→def discretize(trace):\n   300→    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n   301→    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n   302→```\n   303→\n   304→iii. The interpolation and the filter are not choices, they are what the IBL documentation recommends for computing wheel velocity and what `SessionLoader` does by default, so the reference and this conversion get the same trace.\n   305→\n   306→## 7-d. How is `output` *wheel_speed* aligned with the neural data?\n   307→\n   308→i. The wheel trace is sampled at the same 100 bin centres as the neural data, measured from the same stimulus onset, so the two share a time axis bin for bin.\n   309→\n   310→ii. The bin centres, and the wheel evaluated at them:\n   311→```python\n   312→TIME = EDGES[:-1] + BIN / 2                              # bin centres, the first decoder input\n   313→```\n   314→\n   315→```python\n   316→traces.append(np.interp(onset + TIME, times[first:last], values[first:last]))\n   317→```\n   318→\n   319→iii. The wheel is on the same session clock as the spikes, so evaluating it at the bin centres is all the alignment needed.\n   320→\n   321→## 8-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *whisker_motion_energy* derived from?\n   322→\n   323→i. The motion energy of a side camera, `<side>Camera.ROIMotionEnergy` with its frame times `_ibl_<side>Camera.times`. It is a single value per video frame, computed by IBL over a square covering the whisker pad, and is used as released. The left camera is taken when the session has it and the right otherwise.\n   324→\n   325→ii. Choosing the camera, and loading it:\n   326→```python\n   327→def camera_view(eid):\n   328→    \"\"\"The side camera this session has whisker motion energy for, left preferred.\"\"\"\n   329→    for view in ('left', 'right'):\n   330→        if len(one.list_datasets(eid, filename=f'{view}Camera.ROIMotionEnergy.npy',\n   331→                                 query_type='local')):\n   332→            return view\n   333→```\n   334→\n   335→```python\n   336→loader.load_motion_energy(views=[view])\n   337→```\n   338→\n   339→iii. This follows the logic of the reference paer.\n   340→\n   341→## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *whisker_motion_energy*?\n   342→\n   343→i. The released trace is used as it is, with no filtering or normalisation. It is resampled onto the 100 bin centres of each trial and then cut into three classes at the 33rd and 67th percentile of the session, the same rule as the wheel.\n   344→\n   345→ii. Resampling onto the bin centres, then splitting into three:\n   346→```python\n   347→whisker = trial_traces(motion_energy.times, motion_energy.iloc[:, 1], stim_on)\n   348→```\n   349→\n   350→```python\n   351→def discretize(trace):\n   352→    \"\"\"Equal sized classes, split at percentiles of this session's own trace.\"\"\"\n   353→    return np.digitize(trace, np.percentile(trace, SPLIT))\n   354→```\n   355→\n   356→iii. How are **all the data** for all subjects, sessions, and trials loaded in?\n\ni. Everything is read through the ONE api against the IBL cache in `data/one_cache`, never by opening files directly. The index of the brain-wide map release is loaded first, listing the sessions and the datasets each one holds. Searching that index returns an `eid`, the session identifier, and that one string is the only handle needed afterwards: every loader takes the `eid` and resolves the files itself. `SessionLoader` reads the trials table, the wheel and the camera motion energy of a session, and `SpikeSortingLoader` reads the spikes and clusters of one probe insertion, so it is called once per probe.\n\nii. The index, and the search that returns the session identifiers:\n```python\none.load_cache(tag=RELEASE)               # downloads the release index if it is not cached\neids, info = one.search(query_type='local', details=True)\n```\n\nEverything else follows from an `eid`:\n```python\nloader = SessionLoader(one=one, eid=eid)\nloader = SpikeSortingLoader(pid=pid, one=one, eid=eid, pname=name)\n```\n\niii. The search returns the subject name alongside each `eid`, so no parsing of paths or file names is involved. Sessions are collected into a dictionary keyed by subject, which is the form the conversion iterates over. At assembly the subjects are the sorted unique names and `subject_idx` records, for each session, its index into that list. The 444 converted sessions come from 136 subjects, a median of three sessions per animal and up to thirteen.\n\nii. Are the data correctly split into trials?\n\ni. The trials table has one row per trial, so the split is given by the data.\n\nii. The trials table of a session:\n```python\nloader.load_trials()\n```\n\niii. No decision to make, the trials table is already one row per trial.\n\n## 1-e. How are trials filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Four things, all applied together. The reaction time, first wheel movement minus stimulus onset, has to fall between 80 ms and 2 s. Trials where the animal made no choice are dropped. Trials whose choice or block probability is missing are dropped. Finally the trial window has to be spanned by the wheel and the camera, which drops trials at the edges of a recording.\n\nii.\n```python\nkeep = (reaction >= MIN_RT) & (reaction <= MAX_RT)          # NaN compares false, so those go too\nkeep &= trials.choice.isin(CHOICE).to_numpy()               # drops the no response trials\nkeep &= trials.probabilityLeft.isin(PRIOR).to_numpy()\n\nreturn keep & covered(wheel_times, stim_on) & covered(camera_times, stim_on)\n```\n\niii. The reaction time bounds and the no-response exclusion are the reference paper's own trial mask, `min_rt=0.08, max_rt=2` with `exclude_nochoice=True`, and the data paper truncates its reaction time distribution at the same 80 ms and 2 s.\n\n## 2-a. What variables in the raw data is the final `neural` data derived from?\n\ni. Two arrays per probe, `spikes.times` and `spikes.clusters`, which are the time of every spike and the cluster it was assigned to. The cluster table supplies the quality label and the anatomical location, but the neural array itself is built only from those two.\n\nii. Spike time is directly avialible through the loader.\n\n## 2-b. How is the `neural` data processed?\n\ni. Spikes are counted into 20 ms bins over the trial window, giving one count per unit per bin, and the counts are divided by the bin width to become a firing rate in Hz. When a session has two probes their units are pooled into one population, numbered continuously so that the second probe's units continue after the first probe's.\n\nii. Counting a trial, then converting to a rate:\n```python\nbin_index = np.floor((spike_time - T_START) / BIN).astype(np.int64)\nflat_index = spike_unit * N_BINS + bin_index\ncounts[trial] = np.bincount(flat_index, minlength=n_units * N_BINS).reshape(n_units, N_BINS)\n```\n\n```python\nrate = spike_counts(spike_times, spike_units, regions.size, stim_on) / BIN\n```\n\niii. The reference bins spike counts and does not smooth, so no smoothing is applied here either. Merging the probes of a session follows the reference's `merge_probes`, whose reasoning is that two probes in one session see the same behaviour and so are not independent recordings.\n\n## 2-c. How is the `neural` data filtered based on quality controls?\n\ni. Only clusters whose `label` is 1 are kept. The label is a score of 0, 1/3, 2/3 or 1 that the IBL pipeline assigns from a set of spike sorting metrics, so requiring 1 keeps the units that pass all of them. Across the release this keeps 75,708 of 621,733 clusters, and in the converted data 73,044 units over 444 sessions, a median of 142 per session.\n\nii. The filter, applied per probe before the spikes are kept:\n```python\n# spikes.clusters holds the row of the cluster table, so a per cluster flag\n# can be indexed by it directly to say whether each spike survives\nis_good = clusters['label'] >= QC_LABEL\nkeep = is_good[spikes['clusters']]\n```\n\niii. This is the curation of the data paper, which reports 75,708 well-isolated neurons averaging 108 per probe; applying `label >= 1` reproduce their number.\n\n## 2-d. How is the per-trial `neural` data aligned to the event described in the `instructions`?\n\ni. Every stream of a session is already expressed in seconds on one shared clock: spike times, trial event times, wheel timestamps and camera frame times all run from the start of the recording, because IBL synchronises them upstream through a sync channel common to the ephys and the behaviour rig. Aligning a trial is therefore just subtracting its stimulus onset from the spike times, which puts zero at the event and leaves the window running from -0.5 s to 1.5 s around it.\n\nii. The wheel is recorded only when it moves, so `SessionLoader` first interpolates the position onto an even 1000 Hz grid and differentiates it into a velocity, applying a 20 Hz Butterworth low pass while doing so; the speed is the absolute value of that velocity, in radians per second. That trace is then resampled onto the 100 bin centres of each trial. Finally it is cut into three classes at the 33rd and 67th percentile of the session, so the three classes are equally sized within a session.\n\nii. No additional processing.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *whisker_motion_energy* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. choice_to_label(choice_values: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   199→    choice_values = np.asarray(choice_values)\n   200→    if not np.all(np.isin(choice_values, [-1, 1])):\n   201→        bad = trial_number_in_block(probability_left: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   216→    probability_left = np.asarray(probability_left, dtype=float)\n   217→    out = np.zeros(probability_left.shape[0], dtype=np.float32)\n   218→    current = 0\n   219→    prev = None\n   220→    for idx, value in enumerate(probability_left):\n   221→        if idx == 0 or not np.isclose(value, prev):\n   222→            current = 1\n   223→        else:\n   224→            current += 1\n   225→        out[idx] = current\n   226→        prev = value\n   227→    return out\n   228→\n   229→\n   230→def interval_begs: np.ndarray,\n   241→    interval_ends: np.ndarray,\n   242→    interval_len: float,\n   243→    binsize: float,\n   244→) -> np.ndarray:\n   245→    n_intervals = len(interval_begs)\n   246→    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n   247→    cluster_ids = np.unique(clusters)\n   248→    n_clusters = len(cluster_ids)\n   249→    binned_spikes = np.zeros((n_intervals, cluster_ids, cluster_idxs, return_indices=True\n   262→        )\n   263→        binned_spikes[interval_idx, target_indices, :] = binned_tmp[:, :n_bins]\n   264→    return binned_spikes\n   265→\n 403→\n   404→    raise NotImplementedError(target)\n   405→\n   406→\n   407→def get_behavior_per_interval_current(\n   408→    target_times: np.ndarray | None,\n   409→    target_vals: np.ndarray | None,\n   410→    trials_df: pd.DataFrame,\n   411→    allow_nans: bool = True,\n   412→) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | None], np.ndarray, list[str | None]]:\n   413→    binsize = PARAMS[\"binsize\"]\n   414→    start, end = PARAMS[\"time_window\"]\n   415→    interval_len = end - start\n   416→    align_times = trials_df[PARAMS[\"align_time\"]].to_numpy()\n   417→    interval_begs = align_times + start\n   418→    interval_ends = align_times + end\n   419→    n_intervals = len(interval_begs)\n   420→    n_bins = int(np.ceil(interval_len / binsize))\n   421→\n   422→    if target_times is None or target_vals is None:\n   423→        return [None] * n_intervals, np.zeros(n_intervals, dtype=bool), [\"missing\"] * n_intervals\n   424→\n   425→    idxs_beg = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_begs, side=\"right\")\n   426→    idxs_end = np.searchsorted(target_times, interval_ends, side=\"left\")\n   427→    target_times_list = [target_times[ib:ie] for ib, ie in zip(idxs_beg, idxs_end)]\n   428→    target_vals_list = [target_vals[ib:ie] for ib, ie in zip(idxs_beg, idxs_end)]\n   429→\n   430→    interpolated = [None] * n_intervals\n   431→    good = np.zeros(n_intervals, dtype=bool)\n   432→    reasons: list[str | None] = [None] * n_intervals\n   433→\n   434→    for idx, (t_seg, v_seg) in enumerate(zip(target_times_list, target_vals_list)):\n   435→        if len(v_seg) == 0:\n   436→            reasons[idx] = \"target data not present\"\n   437→            continue\n   438→        if np.sum(np.isnan(v_seg)) > 0 and not allow_nans:\n   439→            reasons[idx] = \"nans in target data\"\n   440→            continue\n   441→        if np.isnan(interval_begs[idx]) or np.isnan(interval_ends[idx]):\n   442→            reasons[idx] = \"bad interval data\"\n   443→            continue\n   444→        if np.abs(interval_begs[idx] - t_seg[0]) > binsize:\n   445→            reasons[idx] = \"target data starts too late\"\n   446→            continue\n   447→        if np.abs(interval_ends[idx] - t_seg[-1]) > binsize:\n   448→            reasons[idx] = \"target data ends too early\"\n   449→            continue\n   450→\n   451→        x_interp = np.linspace(interval_begs[idx] + binsize, interval_ends[idx], n_bins)\n   452→        if np.ndim(v_seg) > 1 and v_seg.shape[1] > 1:\n   453→            y_list = []\n   454→            for col in range(v_seg.shape[1]):\n   455→                fn = interp1d(t_seg, v_seg[:, col], kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n   456→                y_list.append(fn(x_interp))\n   457→            y_interp = np.column_stack(y_list)\n   458→        else:\n   459→            fn = interp1d(t_seg, v_seg, kind=\"linear\", fill_value=\"extrapolate\")\n   460→            y_interp = fn(x_interp)\n   461→\n   462→        interpolated[idx] = np.asarray(y_interp, dtype=np.float32)\n   463→        good[idx] = True\n   464→\n   465→    return interpolated, good, reasons\n   466→\n Any]]:\n   518→    if len(probe_info) == 0:\n   519→        raise RuntimeError(\"No probe insertions found\")\n   520→\n   521→    spikes_list = []\n   522→    clusters_list = []\n   523→    for pid, probe_name in probe_info:\n   524→        spikes, clusters = load_spiking_data_current(\n   525→            session_path,\n   526→            probe_name=probe_name,\n   527→            qc=1,\n   528→        )\n   529→        if len(clusters) == 0:\n   530→            continue\n   531→        clusters[\"pid\"] = str(pid)\n   532→        spikes_list.append(spikes)\n   533→        clusters_list.append(clusters)\n   534→\n   535→    if not spikes_list:\n   536→        raise RuntimeError(\"No good clusters after QC filtering\")\n   537→\n   538→    spikes, clusters = merge_probes(spikes_list, clusters_list)\n   539→    neural_dict = {\n   540→        \"spike_times\": spikes[\"times\"],\n   541→        \"spike_clusters\": spikes[\"clusters\"],\n   542→        \"cluster_regions\": clusters[\"acronym\"].to_numpy(),\n   543→    }\n   544→    meta = {\n   545→        \"cluster_regions\": list(clusters[\"acronym\"]),\n   546→        \"good_clusters\": (clusters[\"label\"] >= 1).to_numpy(dtype=np.int8),\n   547→        \"cluster_qc\": {k: np.asarray(v) for k, v in clusters.to_dict(\"list\").items()},\n   548→    }\n   549→    return neural_dict, meta\n   550→\n   551→\n   552→def process_one_session(\n   553→    row: pd.Series,\n   554→    time_axis: np.ndarray,\n   555→) -> ProcessedSession:\n   556→    eid = row[\"eid\"]\n   557→    t0 = time.time()\n   558→    session_path = release_session_path(row)\n   559→\n   560→    trials_df, trials_mask = load_trials_and_mask_current(session_path)\n   561→    t_trials = time.time()\n   562→\n   563→    neural_dict, meta = load_session_neural(session_path, row[\"probe_info\"])\n   564→    reg_clu_ids = np.arange(len(meta[\"cluster_regions\"]), dtype=np.int64)\n   565→    binned_spikes = bin_spiking_data_current(reg_clu_ids, neural_dict, trials_df)\n   566→    t_spikes = time.time()\n   567→\n   568→    wheel_traces, wheel_mask = align_continuous_behavior(session_path, \"wheel-speed\", trials_df)\n   569→    whisker_traces, whisker_mask = align_continuous_behavior(\n   570→        session_path, \"whisker-motion-energy\", trials_df\n   571→    )\n   572→    t_behavior = time.time()\n   573→\n   574→    keep_mask = np.asarray(trials_mask, dtype=bool) & wheel_mask & whisker_mask\n   575→    keep_idx = np.flatnonzero(keep_mask)\n   576→    if keep_idx.size < 2:\n   577→        raise RuntimeError(f\"Only {keep_idx.size} valid trials after filtering\")\n   578→\n   579→    prob_left_all = trials_df[\"probabilityLeft\"].to_numpy(dtype=float)\n   580→    block_num_all = trial_number_in_block(prob_left_all)\n   581→    choice_all = trials_df[\"choice\"].to_numpy()\n   582→\n   583→    neural_trials = [binned_spikes[i].T.astype(np.float32, copy=False) for i in keep_idx]\n   584→    input_trials = [\n   585→        np.vstack(\n   586→            [\n   587→                time_axis,\n   588→                np.full(time_axis.shape[0], block_num_all[i], dtype=np.float32),\n   589→            ]\n   590→        ).astype(np.float32, copy=False)\n   591→        for i in keep_idx\n   592→    ]\n   593→    wheel_aligned = [np.asarray(wheel_traces[i], dtype=np.float32) for i in keep_idx]\n   594→    whisker_aligned = [np.asarray(whisker_traces[i], dtype=np.float32) for i in keep_idx]\n   595→\n   596→    if not all(x.shape == time_axis.shape for x in wheel_aligned):\n   597→        raise RuntimeError(\"Wheel trace shape mismatch after alignment\")\n   598→    if not all(x.shape == time_axis.shape for x in whisker_aligned):\n   599→        raise RuntimeError(\"Whisker trace shape mismatch after alignment\")\n   600→\n   601→    choice_labels = choice_to_label(choice_all[keep_idx])\n   602→    prior_labels = prior_to_label(prob_left_all[keep_idx])\n   603→\n   604→    return ProcessedSession(\n   605→        release_index=int(row[\"release_index\"]),\n   606→        eid=eid,\n   607→        subject=row[\"subject\"],\n   608→        lab=row[\"lab\"],\n   609→        date=row[\"date\"],\n   610→        session_number=int(row[\"session_number\"]),\n   611→        neural=neural_trials,\n   612→        input_data=input_trials,\n   613→        choice_labels=choice_labels,\n   614→        prior_labels=prior_labels,\n   615→        wheel_continuous=wheel_aligned,\n   616→        whisker_continuous=whisker_aligned,\n   617→        cluster_regions=meta[\"cluster_regions\"],\n   618→        cluster_good=np.asarray(meta[\"good_clusters\"], dtype=np.int8),\n   619→        raw_trial_count=len(trials_df),\n   620→        kept_trial_count=len(keep_idx),\n   621→        kept_trial_indices=keep_idx.astype(np.int64),\n   622→        skipped_trial_count=int(len(trials_df) - len(keep_idx)),\n   623→        timing={\n   624→            \"trials_s\": t_trials - t0,\n   625→            \"spikes_s\": t_spikes - t_trials,\n   626→            \"behavior_s\": t_behavior - t_spikes,\n   627→            \"total_s\": t_behavior - t0,\n   628→        },\n   629→    )\n   630→\n   631→\n   632→def process_one_session_worker(row_dict: dict[str, Any], time_axis: np.ndarray) -> ProcessedSession:\n   633→    row = pd.Series(row_dict)\n   634→    last_exc: Exception | None = None\n   635→    for attempt in range(1, 4):\n   636→        try:\n   637→            if attempt > 1:\n   638→                time.sleep(0.5 * attempt + random.random() * 0.5)\n   639→            return process_one_session(row, time_axis)\n   640→        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n   641→            last_exc = exc\n   642→            if attempt >= 3 or not is_likely_transient_error(exc):\n   643→                raise\n   644→    assert last_exc is not None\n   645→    raise last_exc\n   646→\n   647→\n   648→def compute_thresholds(processed_sessions: list[ProcessedSession]) -> dict[str, tuple[float, float]]:\n   649→    wheel_values = np.concatenate(\n   650→        [np.concatenate(session.wheel_continuous) for session in processed_sessions]\n   651→    )\n   652→    whisker_values = np.concatenate(\n   653→        [np.concatenate(session.whisker_continuous) for session in processed_sessions]\n   654→    )\n   655→    thresholds = {}\n   656→    for name, values in [\n   657→        (\"wheel_speed\", wheel_values),\n   658→        (\"whisker_motion_energy\", whisker_values),\n   659→    ]:\n   660→        q1, q2 = np.quantile(values, [1 / 3, 2 / 3])\n   661→        thresholds[name] = (float(q1), float(q2))\n   662→    return thresholds\n   663→\n   664→\n   665→def discretize(values: np.ndarray, thresholds: tuple[float, float]) -> np.ndarray:\n   666→    q1, q2 = thresholds\n   667→    return np.digitize(values, bins=np.array([q1, q2]), right=False).astype(np.int64)\n   668→\n   669→\n   670→def ordered_unique(strings: list[str]) -> list[str]:\n   671→    seen = set()\n   672→    out = []\n   673→    for item in strings:\n   674→        if item not in seen:\n   675→            seen.add(item)\n   676→            out.append(item)\n   677→    return out\n   678→\n   679→\n   680→def to_beryl_acronyms(acronyms: list[str] | np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n   681→    return np.asarray(BRAIN_REGIONS.acronym2acronym(list(acronyms), mapping=\"Beryl\"))\n   682→\n   683→\n   684→def assemble_dataset(\n   685→    processed_sessions: list[ProcessedSession],\n   686→    thresholds: dict[str, tuple[float, float]],\n   687→) -> dict[str, Any]:\n   688→    subjects = ordered_unique([session.subject for session in processed_sessions])\n   689→    subject_to_idx = {subject: idx for idx, subject in enumerate(subjects)}\n   690→\n   691→    session_regions_beryl = [\n   692→        to_beryl_acronyms(session.cluster_regions) for session in processed_sessions\n   693→    ]\n   694→    brain_regions = ordered_unique(\n   695→        [region for regions in session_regions_beryl for region in regions.tolist()]\n   696→    )\n   697→    region_to_idx = {region: idx for idx, region in enumerate(brain_regions)}\n   698→\n   699→    neural = []\n   700→    input_data = []\n   701→    output_data = []\n   702→    subject_idx = []\n   703→    brain_region_idx = []\n   704→\n   705→    for session, session_regions in zip(processed_sessions, session_regions_beryl):\n   706→        neural.append(session.neural)\n   707→        input_data.append(session.input_data)\n   708→        outputs_session = []\n   709→        for trial_idx in range(session.kept_trial_count):\n   710→            T = session.neural[trial_idx].shape[1]\n   711→            outputs_session.append(\n   712→                np.vstack(\n   713→                    [\n   714→                        np.full(T, session.choice_labels[trial_idx], dtype=np.int64),\n   715→                        np.full(T, session.prior_labels[trial_idx], dtype=np.int64),\n   716→                        discretize(\n   717→                            session.wheel_continuous[trial_idx],\n   718→                            thresholds[\"wheel_speed\"],\n   719→                        ),\n   720→                        discretize(\n   721→                            session.whisker_continuous[trial_idx],\n   722→                            thresholds[\"whisker_motion_energy\"],\n   723→                        ),\n   724→                    ]\n   725→                )\n   726→            )\n   727→        output_data.append(outputs_session)\n   728→        subject_idx.append(subject_to_idx[session.subject])\n   729→        brain_region_idx.append(\n   730→            np.array(\n   731→                [region_to_idx[region] for region in session_regions.tolist()],\n   732→                dtype=np.int64,\n   733→            )\n   734→        )\n   735→\n   736→    metadata = {\n   737→        \"task_description\": (\n   738→            \"IBL visual decision task converted to a stimulus-onset aligned trial dataset. \"\n   739→            \"Neural inputs are well-isolated spike counts; decoder targets are choice, prior \"\n   740→            \"probability of left, wheel-speed bins, and whisker-motion-energy bins.\"\n   741→        ),\n   742→        \"time_bin_size\": 20.0,\n   743→        \"temporal_alignment_event\": \"stimulus onset (stimOn_times)\",\n   744→        \"off_start\": float(PARAMS[\"time_window\"][0]),\n   745→        \"off_end\": float(PARAMS[\"time_window\"][1]),\n   746→        \"time_axis_definition\": \"bin end times relative to stimulus onset\",\n   747→        \"source_release\": \"code/code_zhang2025/data/bwm_release.csv\",\n   748→        \"choice_mapping\": {\"1\": \"left\", \"-1\": \"right\"},\n   749→        \"prior_mapping\": {\"0.2\": 0, \"0.5\": 1, \"0.8\": 2},\n   750→        \"wheel_speed_thresholds\": thresholds[\"wheel_speed\"],\n   751→        \"whisker_motion_energy_thresholds\": thresholds[\"whisker_motion_energy\"],\n   752→        \"brain_region_mapping\": \"Beryl\",\n   753→        \"n_sessions_attempted\": len(processed_sessions),\n   754→        \"session_eids\": [session.eid for session in processed_sessions],\n   755→    }\n   756→\n   757→    return {\n   758→        \"neural\": neural,\n   759→        \"input\": input_data,\n   760→        \"output\": \"brain_region_idx\": brain_region_idx,\n   765→        \"input_names\": INPUT_NAMES,\n   766→        \"output_names\": OUTPUT_NAMES,\n   767→        \"output_values\": OUTPUT_VALUES,\n   768→        \"metadata\": metadata,\n   769→    }\n   770→\n   771→\n   772→def plot_processing(session: ProcessedSession, thresholds: dict[str, tuple[float, float]]) -> None:\n   773→    example_trial = 0\n str:\n   863→    units = [\"B\", \"KB\", \"MB\", \"GB\", \"TB\"]\n   864→    size = float(num_bytes)\n   865→    for unit in units:\n   866→        if size < 1024.0 or unit == units[-1]:\n   867→            return f\"{size:.2f} {unit}\"\n   868→        size /= 1024.0\n   869→    return f\"{num_bytes} B\"\n   870→\n   871→\n   872→def main() -> None:\n   873→    args = parse_args()\n   874→    mode = \"sample\" if args.sample else \"full\"\n   875→    time_axis = build_time_axis()\n   876→\n   877→    bwm_df, sessions_df = load_release_sessions()\n   878→    log(f\"Loaded release roster: {len(sessions_df)} sessions from {BWM_RELEASE_CSV.name}\")\n   879→    sessions_df = sessions_df.reset_index(names=\"release_index\")\n   880→\n   881→    if mode == \"sample\":\n   882→        target_successes = 2\n   883→        candidate_rows = sessions_df.itertuples(index=False)\n   884→    else:\n   885→        target_successes = None\n   886→        candidate_rows = sessions_df.itertuples(index=False)\n   887→\n   888→    processed_sessions: list[ProcessedSession] = []\n   889→    skipped_sessions: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n   890→\n   891→    total_start = time.time()\n   892→    if mode == \"sample\":\n   893→        for idx, row in enumerate(candidate_rows, start=1):\n   894→            row_series = pd.Series(row._asdict())\n   895→            log(\n   896→                f\"[{idx}/{len(sessions_df)}] Processing {row_series['eid']} \"\n   897→                f\"({row_series['subject']} {row_series['date']} #{int(row_series['session_number'])})\"\n   898→            )\n   899→            try:\n   900→                session = process_one_session(row_series, time_axis)\n   901→                processed_sessions.append(session)\n   902→                log(\n   903→                    f\"  kept {session.kept_trial_count}/{session.raw_trial_count} trials, \"\n   904→                    f\"{len(session.cluster_regions)} neurons, total {session.timing['total_s']:.1f}s\"\n   905→                )\n   906→                if target_successes is not None and len(processed_sessions) >= target_successes:\n   907→                    break\n   908→            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n   909→                skipped_sessions.append(\n   910→                    {\n   911→                        \"eid\": row_series[\"eid\"],\n   912→                        \"subject\": row_series[\"subject\"],\n   913→                        \"error\": str(exc),\n   914→                        \"traceback\": traceback.format_exc(),\n   915→                        \"has_local_modalities\": session_has_local_modalities(row_series),\n   916→                    }\n   917→                )\n   918→                log(f\"  skipped: {exc}\")\n   919→    else:\n   920→        records = sessions_df.to_dict(orient=\"records\")\n   921→        retry_records: list[dict[str, Any]] = []\n   922→        log(f\"Using {args.session_workers} session workers for full conversion\")\n   923→        with ProcessPoolExecutor(max_workers=args.session_workers) as executor:\n   924→            future_map = {\n   925→                executor.submit(process_one_session_worker, row_dict, time_axis): row_dict\n   926→                for row_dict in records\n   927→            }\n   928→            completed = 0\n   929→            total = len(future_map)\n   930→            for future in as_completed(future_map):\n   931→                row_dict = future_map[future]\n   932→                completed += 1\n   933→                try:\n   934→                    session = future.result()\n   935→                    processed_sessions.append(session)\n   936→                    log(\n   937→                        f\"[{completed}/{total}] kept {session.eid}: \"\n   938→                        f\"{session.kept_trial_count}/{session.raw_trial_count} trials, \"\n   939→                        f\"{len(session.cluster_regions)} neurons, {session.timing['total_s']:.1f}s\"\n   940→                    )\n   941→                except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n   942→                    retry_records.append(row_dict)\n   943→                    log(\n   944→                        f\"[{completed}/{total}] worker failed {row_dict['eid']}: {exc} \"\n   945→                        f\"(queued for sequential retry)\"\n   946→                    )\n   947→\n   948→        if retry_records:\n   949→            log(f\"Sequential retry pass for {len(retry_records)} session(s)\")\n   950→            for idx, row_dict in enumerate(retry_records, start=1):\n   951→                row_series = pd.Series(row_dict)\n   952→                try:\n   953→                    session = process_one_session(row_series, time_axis)\n   954→                    processed_sessions.append(session)\n   955→                    log(\n   956→                        f\"[retry {idx}/{len(retry_records)}] kept {session.eid}: \"\n   957→                        f\"{session.kept_trial_count}/{session.raw_trial_count} trials, \"\n   958→                        f\"{len(session.cluster_regions)} neurons, {session.timing['total_s']:.1f}s\"\n   959→                    )\n   960→                except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n   961→                    skipped_sessions.append(\n   962→                        {\n   963→                            \"eid\": row_dict[\"eid\"],\n   964→                            \"subject\": row_dict[\"subject\"],\n   965→                            \"error\": str(exc),\n   966→                            \"traceback\": traceback.format_exc(),\n   967→                            \"has_local_modalities\": session_has_local_modalities(pd.Series(row_dict)),\n   968→                        }\n   969→                    )\n   970→                    log(f\"[retry {idx}/{len(retry_records)}] skipped {row_dict['eid']}: {exc}\")\n   971→\n   972→    if not processed_sessions:\n   973→        raise RuntimeError(\"No sessions were successfully converted\")\n   974→\n   975→    processed_sessions.sort(key=lambda session: session.release_index)\n   976→\n   977→    thresholds = compute_thresholds(processed_sessions)\n   978→    log(\n   979→        \"Discretization thresholds: \"\n   980→        f\"wheel={thresholds['wheel_speed']}, \"\n   981→        f\"whisker={thresholds['whisker_motion_energy']}\"\n   982→    )\n   983→\n   984→    dataset = assemble_dataset(processed_sessions, thresholds)\n   985→    dataset[\"metadata\"][\"mode\"] = mode\n   986→    dataset[\"metadata\"][\"n_sessions_attempted\"] = (\n   987→        len(processed_sessions) + len(skipped_sessions)\n   988→    )\n   989→    dataset[\"metadata\"][\"n_sessions_converted\"] = len(processed_sessions)\n   990→    dataset[\"metadata\"][\"skipped_sessions\"] = [\n   991→        {\n   992→            \"eid\": item[\"eid\"],\n   993→            \"subject\": item[\"subject\"],\n   994→            \"error\": get_behavior_per_interval_current(\n    target_times: np.ndarray | None,\n    target_vals: np.ndarray | None,\n    trials_df: pd.DataFrame,\n    allow_nans: bool = True,\n) -> tuple[list[np.ndarray | interpolated[idx] = np.asarray(y_interp, dtype=np.float32)\n        good[idx] = True\n\n    return interpolated, good, reasons\n\n\ndef align_continuous_behavior(\n    session_path: Path,\n
